					<div id="contentSub"><div id="mw-content-subtitle"><span class="mw-redirectedfrom">(Redirected from <a href="/w/index.php?title=James,_son_of_Zebedee&amp;redirect=no" class="mw-redirect" title="James, son of Zebedee">James, son of Zebedee</a>)</span></div></div>
					
					
					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">One of the Twelve Apostles of Jesus</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Not to be confused with <a href="/wiki/James_the_Less,_Son_of_Alpheus" class="mw-redirect" title="James the Less, Son of Alpheus">James the Less, Son of Alpheus</a>.</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">"St. Jacob" and "St James the Great" redirect here. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/St._Jacob_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="St. Jacob (disambiguation)">St. Jacob (disambiguation)</a> and <a href="/wiki/St_James_the_Great_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="St James the Great (disambiguation)">St James the Great (disambiguation)</a>.</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>

<a href="/wiki/Guayaquil" title="Guayaquil">Guayaquil</a>, <a href="/wiki/Betis_Church" title="Betis Church">Betis Church</a>, Guagua, <a href="/wiki/Pampanga" title="Pampanga">Pampanga</a>, <a <p><b>James the Great</b> was one of the <a href="/wiki/Apostles_in_the_New_Testament" title="Apostles in the New Testament">Twelve Apostles</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jesus" title="Jesus">Jesus</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament">New Testament</a>, he was the second of the apostles to die (after <a href="/wiki/Judas_Iscariot" title="Judas Iscariot">Judas Iscariot</a>), and the first to be <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">martyred</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bibleverse&#124;Acts&#124;12:2_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bibleverse|Acts|12:2-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Saint James is the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a> and, according to tradition, his remains are held in <a href="/wiki/Santiago_de_Compostela" title="Santiago de Compostela">Santiago de Compostela</a> in <a href="/wiki/Galicia_(Spain)" title="Galicia (Spain)">Galicia</a>. He is also known as <b>James, son of Zebedee</b>, <b>Saint James the Great</b>, <b>Saint James the Greater</b>, <b>Saint James the Elder</b>, or <b>Saint Jacob</b> (<a href="/wiki/Aramaic" title="Aramaic">Aramaic</a> ܝܥܩܘܒ ܒܪ ܙܒܕܝ,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <a href="/wiki/Arabic" title="Arabic">Arabic</a> يعقوب; <a href="/wiki/Biblical_Hebrew" title="Biblical Hebrew">Hebrew</a> בן זבדי <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1020748213">.mw-parser-output .script-hebrew,.mw-parser-output .script-Hebr{font-family:"SBL Hebrew","SBL BibLit","Taamey Ashkenaz","Taamey Frank CLM","Frank Ruehl CLM","Ezra SIL","Ezra SIL SR","Keter Aram Tsova","Taamey David CLM","Keter YG","Shofar","David CLM","Hadasim CLM","Simple CLM","Nachlieli",Cardo,Alef,"Noto Serif Hebrew","Noto Sans Hebrew","David Libre",David,"Times New Roman",Gisha,Arial,FreeSerif,FreeSans}</style><span class="script-hebrew" style="font-size: 115%;" dir="rtl">יַעֲקֹב</span>&#8206;,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Jacob_(name)" title="Jacob (name)">Yaʿăqōb</a>,</i> <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a> <i>Iacobus Maior</i>, <a href="/wiki/Koine_Greek" title="Koine Greek">Greek</a> Ἰάκωβος τοῦ Ζεβεδαίου <i>Iákōbos tû Zebedaíou</i>;<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> died <a href="/wiki/Anno_Domini" title="Anno Domini">AD</a> 44).
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:James_the_Great_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena,_Italy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/James_the_Great_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/220px-James_the_Great_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/James_the_Great_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/330px-James_the_Great_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/James_the_Great_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/440px-James_the_Great_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1901" data-file-height="1267" /></a><figcaption>James the Apostle, detail of the mosaic in the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_San_Vitale" title="Basilica of San Vitale">Basilica of San Vitale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ravenna" title="Ravenna">Ravenna</a>, 6th century</figcaption></figure>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_the_New_Testament">In the New Testament</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: In the New Testament">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>James was the son of <a href="/wiki/Zebedee" title="Zebedee">Zebedee</a> and <a href="/wiki/Salome_(disciple)" title="Salome (disciple)">Salome</a>. Salome was a sister of Mary (mother of Jesus) which made James the Great cousin of Jesus. James is styled "the Greater" to distinguish him from the Apostle <a href="/wiki/James_the_Less" title="James the Less">James "the Less,"</a> with "greater" meaning older or taller, rather than more important. James the Great was the brother of <a href="/wiki/John_the_Apostle" title="John the Apostle">John the Apostle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECamerlynck1910_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECamerlynck1910-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>James is described as one of the first disciples to join Jesus. The <a href="/wiki/Synoptic_Gospels" title="Synoptic Gospels">Synoptic Gospels</a> state that James and John were with their father by the seashore when Jesus called them to follow him.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>James, along with his brother John and <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Peter</a>, formed an informal <a href="/wiki/Triumvirate" title="Triumvirate">triumvirate</a> among the <a href="/wiki/Apostles_in_the_New_Testament" title="Apostles in the New Testament">Twelve Apostles</a>. Jesus allowed them to be the only apostles present at three particular occasions during his public ministry, the <a href="/wiki/Raising_of_Jairus%27_daughter" title="Raising of Jairus&#39; daughter">raising of Jairus' daughter</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Transfiguration_of_Jesus" title="Transfiguration of Jesus">transfiguration of Jesus</a><sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Agony_in_the_Garden" title="Agony in the Garden">agony in the Garden of Gethsemane</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> James and John<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> (or, in another tradition, <a href="/wiki/Salome_(disciple)" title="Salome (disciple)">their mother</a><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>) asked Jesus to grant them seats on his right and left in his glory. Jesus rebuked them, asking if they were ready to drink from the cup he was going to drink from and saying the honor was not even for him to grant. The other apostles were annoyed with them. James and his brother wanted to call down fire on a <a href="/wiki/Samaritans" title="Samaritans">Samaritan</a> town, but were rebuked by Jesus.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Shield_with_symbol_of_St._James_the_Great,_Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_(Rosemont,_Pennsylvania).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Shield_with_symbol_of_St._James_the_Great%2C_Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_%28Rosemont%2C_Pennsylvania%29.jpg/220px-Shield_with_symbol_of_St._James_the_Great%2C_Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_%28Rosemont%2C_Pennsylvania%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Shield_with_symbol_of_St._James_the_Great%2C_Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_%28Rosemont%2C_Pennsylvania%29.jpg/330px-Shield_with_symbol_of_St._James_the_Great%2C_Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_%28Rosemont%2C_Pennsylvania%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Shield_with_symbol_of_St._James_the_Great%2C_Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_%28Rosemont%2C_Pennsylvania%29.jpg/440px-Shield_with_symbol_of_St._James_the_Great%2C_Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_%28Rosemont%2C_Pennsylvania%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1536" data-file-height="2048" /></a><figcaption>Shield with symbol of St. James the Great, <a href="/wiki/Church_of_the_Good_Shepherd_(Rosemont,_Pennsylvania)" title="Church of the Good Shepherd (Rosemont, Pennsylvania)">Church of the Good Shepherd (Rosemont, Pennsylvania)</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Acts_of_the_Apostles" title="Acts of the Apostles">Acts of the Apostles</a> records that "Herod the king" (usually identified with <a href="/wiki/Herod_Agrippa" title="Herod Agrippa">Herod Agrippa</a>) had James executed by the sword.<sup id="cite_ref-bibleverse&#124;Acts&#124;12:2_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bibleverse|Acts|12:2-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Nixon suggests that this may have been caused by James's fiery temper,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENixon19631354_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENixon19631354-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> in which he and his brother earned the nickname <i><a href="/wiki/Language_of_Jesus#Boanerges_(Βοανηργές)" title="Language of Jesus">Boanerges</a></i> or "Sons of Thunder".<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/F._F._Bruce" title="F. F. Bruce">F. F. Bruce</a> contrasts this story to that of the <a href="/wiki/Liberation_of_Peter" title="Liberation of Peter">Liberation of Saint Peter</a>, and writes that the proposition that "James should die while Peter should escape" is a "mystery of <a href="/wiki/Divine_providence" title="Divine providence">divine providence</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBruce1964237_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBruce1964237-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration">Veneration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Veneration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rembrandt_-_Sankt_Jakobus_der_%C3%84ltere.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Rembrandt_-_Sankt_Jakobus_der_%C3%84ltere.jpg/220px-Rembrandt_-_Sankt_Jakobus_der_%C3%84ltere.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="267" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Rembrandt_-_Sankt_Jakobus_der_%C3%84ltere.jpg/330px-Rembrandt_-_Sankt_Jakobus_der_%C3%84ltere.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Rembrandt_-_Sankt_Jakobus_der_%C3%84ltere.jpg/440px-Rembrandt_-_Sankt_Jakobus_der_%C3%84ltere.jpg 2x" data-file-width="613" data-file-height="745" /></a><figcaption><i>Saint James the Elder</i> was painted by <a href="/wiki/Rembrandt" title="Rembrandt">Rembrandt</a> in 1661. He is depicted clothed as a <a href="/wiki/Pilgrim" title="Pilgrim">pilgrim</a>, with a <a href="/wiki/Shell_of_Saint_James" class="mw-redirect" title="Shell of Saint James">scallop</a> shell on his shoulder, and his staff and <a href="/wiki/Pilgrim%27s_hat" title="Pilgrim&#39;s hat">pilgrim's hat</a> beside him.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In the Catholic tradition, Saint James is the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a> and, according to legend, his remains are held in <a href="/wiki/Santiago_de_Compostela" title="Santiago de Compostela">Santiago de Compostela</a> in <a href="/wiki/Galicia_(Spain)" title="Galicia (Spain)">Galicia</a>. This name <i>Santiago</i> is the local evolution of Latin <a href="/wiki/Genitive" class="mw-redirect" title="Genitive">genitive</a> <i>Sancti Iacobi</i>, "(church or sanctuary) <i>of Saint James</i>" (evolved into a personal name in Spanish, and also in Portuguese -<i>Tiago</i>-, with its derivatives <i>Diego</i>/<i>Diogo</i>). The traditional <a href="/wiki/Christian_pilgrimage" title="Christian pilgrimage">pilgrimage</a> to the grave of the saint, known as the "<a href="/wiki/Way_of_St._James" class="mw-redirect" title="Way of St. James">Way of St. James</a>", has been the most popular pilgrimage for Western European Catholics from the <a href="/wiki/Early_Middle_Ages" title="Early Middle Ages">Early Middle Ages</a> onwards, although its modern revival and popularity stem from <a href="/wiki/Walter_Starkie" title="Walter Starkie">Walter Starkie</a>'s 1957 book, <i>The Road to Santiago. The Pilgrims of St. James</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStarkie1957_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStarkie1957-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Officially, 327,378 pilgrims registered in 2018 as having completed the final 100 kilometres (62&#160;mi) walk (200 kilometres (120&#160;mi) by bicycle) to Santiago to qualify for a <a href="/wiki/Camino_de_Santiago#Compostela" title="Camino de Santiago">Compostela</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cathedral_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cathedral-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> When 25 July falls on a Sunday, it is a "Holy Year" (an <a href="/wiki/Jacobean_holy_year" class="mw-redirect" title="Jacobean holy year">Jacobean holy year</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/A%C3%B1o_santo_jacobeo" class="extiw" title="es:Año santo jacobeo">es</a>&#93;</span>) and a special east door is opened for entrance into Santiago Cathedral. Jubilee years follow a 6-5-6-11 pattern (except when the last year of a century is not a leap year, which can yield a gap of 7 or 12 years). In the 2004 Holy Year, 179,944<sup id="cite_ref-archicompostela_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-archicompostela-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> pilgrims were received at Compostela. In the 2010 Holy Year the number had risen to 272,412.<sup id="cite_ref-peregrinossantiago_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-peregrinossantiago-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The most recent such Holy Year was 2021; the next will be 2027.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Feast">Feast</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Feast">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Feast_of_Saint_James" title="Feast of Saint James">Feast of Saint James</a></div>
<p>The feast day of St. James is celebrated on 25 July on the <a href="/wiki/Liturgical_calendar" class="mw-redirect" title="Liturgical calendar">liturgical calendars</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lutheran" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran">Lutheran</a> and certain other <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> churches. He is commemorated on 30 April in the <a href="/wiki/Orthodox_Christian_liturgical_calendar" class="mw-redirect" title="Orthodox Christian liturgical calendar">Orthodox Christian liturgical calendar</a> (for those churches which follow the traditional <a href="/wiki/Julian_Calendar" class="mw-redirect" title="Julian Calendar">Julian Calendar</a>, 30 April currently falls on 13 May of the modern <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_Calendar" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregorian Calendar">Gregorian Calendar</a>). The <i><a href="/wiki/National_day" title="National day">national day</a></i> of Galicia is also celebrated on 25 July: St James is its patron saint.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>James the Apostle is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Festival_(Anglicanism)" title="Festival (Anglicanism)">Festival</a> on 25 July.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Jerusalem">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The site of martyrdom is located within the <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_of_Saint_James,_Jerusalem" title="Cathedral of Saint James, Jerusalem">Armenian Apostolic Cathedral of St. James</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_Quarter" title="Armenian Quarter">Armenian Quarter</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a>. The Chapel of St. James the Great, located to the left of the sanctuary, is the traditional place where he was martyred, when King Agrippa ordered him to be beheaded (Acts 12:1–2). His head is believed to be buried under the altar, marked by a piece of red marble and surrounded by six votive lamps.<sup id="cite_ref-christusrex_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-christusrex-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Spain">Spain</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Spain">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mission_in_Spain_and_burial_at_Compostela">Mission in Spain and burial at Compostela</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Mission in Spain and burial at Compostela">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Camino_de_Santiago" title="Camino de Santiago">Camino de Santiago</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Santiago.de.Compostela.Catedral.Noche.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Santiago.de.Compostela.Catedral.Noche.jpg/220px-Santiago.de.Compostela.Catedral.Noche.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Santiago.de.Compostela.Catedral.Noche.jpg/330px-Santiago.de.Compostela.Catedral.Noche.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Santiago.de.Compostela.Catedral.Noche.jpg/440px-Santiago.de.Compostela.Catedral.Noche.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="3072" /></a><figcaption>According to Catholic tradition, James preached <a href="/wiki/Evangelism" title="Evangelism">the message and teachings of Jesus Christ</a> in Hispania. In AD&#160;44, he was beheaded in Jerusalem and his remains were later transferred to Galicia in a stone boat, to the place where <a href="/wiki/Santiago_de_Compostela_Cathedral" title="Santiago de Compostela Cathedral">Santiago de Compostela Cathedral</a> now stands.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The 12th century <i><a href="/wiki/Historia_Compostelana" title="Historia Compostelana">Historia Compostelana</a></i> commissioned by <a href="/wiki/Diego_Gelm%C3%ADrez" title="Diego Gelmírez">Diego Gelmírez</a> provides a summary of the legend of St. James, as it was believed at Compostela at that time. Two propositions are central to the legend: first, that James preached the gospel in <a href="/wiki/Hispania" title="Hispania">Hispania</a> as well as in the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Land" title="Holy Land">Holy Land</a>; second, that after his <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">martyrdom</a> at the hands of <a href="/wiki/Herod_Agrippa" title="Herod Agrippa">Herod Agrippa</a>, his followers carried his body by sea to Hispania, where they landed at <a href="/wiki/Padr%C3%B3n" title="Padrón">Padrón</a> on the coast of <a href="/wiki/Gallaecia" title="Gallaecia">Galicia</a>, then carried it over land for burial at <a href="/wiki/Santiago_de_Compostela" title="Santiago de Compostela">Santiago de Compostela</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Esparza_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Esparza-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to ancient local tradition, on 2 January AD 40, the <a href="/wiki/Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Virgin Mary">Virgin Mary</a> appeared to James on the bank of the <a href="/wiki/Ebro_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Ebro River">Ebro River</a> at <a href="/wiki/Caesaraugusta" class="mw-redirect" title="Caesaraugusta">Caesaraugusta</a>, while he was preaching the Gospel in <a href="/wiki/Hispania" title="Hispania">Hispania</a>. She <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Pillar" title="Our Lady of the Pillar">appeared upon a pillar</a>, and that pillar is conserved and venerated within the present <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Our_Lady_of_the_Pillar" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of Our Lady of the Pillar">Basilica of Our Lady of the Pillar</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Zaragoza" title="Zaragoza">Zaragoza</a>, Spain. Following that apparition, St. James returned to <a href="/wiki/Judaea_(Roman_province)" title="Judaea (Roman province)">Judaea</a>, where he was beheaded by <a href="/wiki/Herod_Agrippa_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Herod Agrippa I">Herod Agrippa I</a> in AD 44.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChadwick1976_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChadwick1976-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFletcher1984_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFletcher1984-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Translation_(relics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Translation (relics)">translation</a> of his relics from <a href="/wiki/Judaea_(Roman_province)" title="Judaea (Roman province)">Judaea</a> to Galicia in the northwest of Hispania was, in legend, accomplished by a series of miraculous events: his decapitated body was taken up by angels and sailed in a rudderless, unattended boat to <a href="/wiki/Iria_Flavia" title="Iria Flavia">Iria Flavia</a> in Hispania, where a massive rock closed around his body.<sup id="cite_ref-Esparza_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Esparza-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Galician_mythology&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Galician mythology (page does not exist)">Tradition</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://gl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitolox%C3%ADa_galega" class="extiw" title="gl:Mitoloxía galega">gl</a>&#93;</span> has it that when the disciples of the James, Theodore and Athanasius, arrived in <a href="/wiki/Iria_Flavia" title="Iria Flavia">Iria Flavia</a>, they approached <a href="/wiki/Queen_Lupa" title="Queen Lupa">Queen Lupa</a> about giving them a place to bury his body.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Lupa appears in the <i><a href="/wiki/Codex_Calixtinus" title="Codex Calixtinus">Codex Calixtinus</a></i> which further relates that she decides to trick the disciples and sends them to the governor of <a href="/w/index.php?title=Duio_(San_Vicenzo)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Duio (San Vicenzo) (page does not exist)">Duio</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://gl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duio,_Fisterra" class="extiw" title="gl:Duio, Fisterra">gl</a>&#93;</span> with the intent of having them killed. Sensing a trap, they escape the governor and return to the queen. Once again Lupa tries to deceive them and sends them to <a href="/wiki/Pico_Sacro" title="Pico Sacro">Pico Sacro</a> (the Sacred Peak) to collect two of her oxen to carry the necessary material to build the tomb. She does not tell them that the mountain has a cave which is the entrance to <a href="/wiki/Hell_in_Christianity" title="Hell in Christianity">hell</a> and is guarded by a dragon. However the presence of the <a href="/wiki/Christian_cross" title="Christian cross">holy cross</a> protects the disciples from harm and tames the bulls.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-translatiomedia_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-translatiomedia-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Upon witnessing the miraculous events, Lupa converts to <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> and helps build the apostle's tomb in <a href="/wiki/Libredon" class="mw-redirect" title="Libredon">Libredon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The tradition at Compostela placed the discovery of the relics of the saint in the 9th century, by <a href="/wiki/Pelagius_the_Hermit" title="Pelagius the Hermit">Pelayo</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Libredon" class="mw-redirect" title="Libredon">Libredon</a> forest in the time of <a href="/wiki/Theodemir_of_Iria" title="Theodemir of Iria">Bishop Theodemir</a> and king <a href="/wiki/Alfonso_II_of_Asturias" title="Alfonso II of Asturias">Alfonso II</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Franco_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franco-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> These traditions were the basis for the pilgrimage route that began to be established in the 9th century, and the shrine dedicated to James at <a href="/wiki/Santiago_de_Compostela" title="Santiago de Compostela">Santiago de Compostela</a> became a famous pilgrimage site within the Christian world. The <a href="/wiki/Way_of_St._James" class="mw-redirect" title="Way of St. James">Way of St. James</a> is a network of routes that cross Western Europe and arrive at Santiago de Compostela through northern Spain.<sup id="cite_ref-Pierced_Hearts_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pierced_Hearts-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Medieval_.22Santiago_Matamoros.22_legend"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Medieval_&quot;Santiago_Matamoros&quot;_legend">Medieval "Santiago Matamoros" legend</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Medieval &quot;Santiago Matamoros&quot; legend">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Saint_James_Matamoros" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint James Matamoros">Saint James Matamoros</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo_-_St_Jacobus_in_Budapest.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo_-_St_Jacobus_in_Budapest.jpg/170px-Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo_-_St_Jacobus_in_Budapest.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="337" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo_-_St_Jacobus_in_Budapest.jpg/255px-Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo_-_St_Jacobus_in_Budapest.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo_-_St_Jacobus_in_Budapest.jpg/340px-Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo_-_St_Jacobus_in_Budapest.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1206" data-file-height="2390" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Saint_James_Matamoros" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint James Matamoros">Saint James as the Moor-killer</a> by <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Tiepolo" title="Giovanni Battista Tiepolo">Giovanni Battista Tiepolo</a> (<a href="/wiki/Museum_of_Fine_Arts_(Budapest)" title="Museum of Fine Arts (Budapest)">Museum of Fine Arts</a>, <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a>). His mantle is that of his military order.</figcaption></figure>
<p>An even later tradition states that he miraculously appeared to fight for the Christian army during the legendary <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Clavijo" title="Battle of Clavijo">battle of Clavijo</a>, and was henceforth called <i>Santiago Matamoros</i> (Saint James the <a href="/wiki/Moors" title="Moors">Moor</a>-slayer). <i><a href="/wiki/Santiago_y_cierra_Espa%C3%B1a" class="mw-redirect" title="Santiago y cierra España">¡Santiago, y cierra, España!</a></i> ("St. James and strike for Spain") was the traditional <a href="/wiki/Battle_cry" title="Battle cry">battle cry</a> of medieval Spanish (Christian) armies. <a href="/wiki/Miguel_de_Cervantes" title="Miguel de Cervantes">Miguel de Cervantes</a> has <a href="/wiki/Don_Quixote" title="Don Quixote">Don Quixote</a> explaining that "the great knight of the russet cross was given by God to Spain as patron and protector".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECervantes1863441_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECervantes1863441-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A similar miracle is related to <a href="/wiki/Emilian_of_Cogolla" class="mw-redirect" title="Emilian of Cogolla">San Millán</a>. The possibility that a cult of James was instituted to supplant the Galician cult of <a href="/wiki/Priscillian" title="Priscillian">Priscillian</a> (executed in 385) who was widely venerated across the north of Iberia as a martyr (at the hands of the local bishops, rather than as a heretic) should not be overlooked. This was cautiously raised by Henry Chadwick in his book on Priscillian;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChadwick1976_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChadwick1976-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> it is not the traditional Roman Catholic view. The <i>Catholic Encyclopedia</i> of 1908, however, is quite cautious about the origins of the cult (see above at <a href="#Controversy">"Controversy"</a>).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Emblem">Emblem</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Emblem">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cross_Santiago.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Cross_Santiago.svg/170px-Cross_Santiago.svg.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="231" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Cross_Santiago.svg/255px-Cross_Santiago.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Cross_Santiago.svg/340px-Cross_Santiago.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="750" data-file-height="1019" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Cross_of_Saint_James" title="Cross of Saint James">Cross of Saint James</a>, the symbol of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Santiago" title="Order of Santiago">Order of Santiago</a>; the hilt is surmounted with a scallop.</figcaption></figure>
<p>James' emblem was the <a href="/wiki/Shell_of_Saint_James" class="mw-redirect" title="Shell of Saint James">scallop</a> shell (or "<a href="/wiki/Cockle_(bivalve)" title="Cockle (bivalve)">cockle</a> shell"), and pilgrims to his shrine often wore that symbol on their hats or clothes. The French term for a scallop is <i>coquille St. Jacques</i>, which means "cockle (or mollusc) of [St.] Jacob". The German word for a scallop is <i>Jakobsmuschel</i>, which means "Jacob's mussel (or clam)"; the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a> word is <i>Jacobsschelp</i>, meaning "Jacob's shell". In <a href="/wiki/Danish_language" title="Danish language">Danish</a> and with the same meaning as in Dutch, the word is <i>Ibskal</i> – <i>Ib</i> being a Danish version of the name "Jakob" and <i>skal</i> meaning "shell".<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_Order_of_Santiago">Military Order of Santiago</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Military Order of Santiago">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The military <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Santiago" title="Order of Santiago">Order of Santiago</a>, named after Saint Tiago or Saint James, was founded in Spain in the 12th century to fight the <a href="/wiki/Moors" title="Moors">Moors</a>. Later, as in other <a href="/wiki/Order_of_chivalry" title="Order of chivalry">orders of chivalry</a>, the membership became a mark of honor.<sup id="cite_ref-Billington_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Billington-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Latter-day_Saints">Latter-day Saints</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Latter-day Saints">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</a> teaches that in 1829 the Apostles James, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Peter</a> and <a href="/wiki/John_the_Apostle" title="John the Apostle">John</a> appeared as heavenly messengers to <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Smith" title="Joseph Smith">Joseph Smith</a> and <a href="/wiki/Oliver_Cowdery" title="Oliver Cowdery">Oliver Cowdery</a> and conferred upon them the <a href="/wiki/Melchizedek_priesthood_(Latter_Day_Saints)" title="Melchizedek priesthood (Latter Day Saints)">Melchizedek priesthood</a> authority of <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_succession" title="Apostolic succession">apostolic succession</a>, and thus exclusively on earth to their organization.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the teaching, this occurred some time after May 15th, 1829 when <a href="/wiki/John_the_Baptist" title="John the Baptist">John the Baptist</a> similarly appeared to Smith and Cowdery and conferred upon them the <a href="/wiki/Aaronic_priesthood_(Latter_Day_Saints)" title="Aaronic priesthood (Latter Day Saints)">Aaronic</a>, or lesser, priesthood, stating that he was doing so under the direction of James, Peter and John.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_Islam">In Islam</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: In Islam">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Quran" title="Quran">Quranic</a> account of the <a href="/wiki/Disciple_(Christianity)" title="Disciple (Christianity)">disciples</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jesus_in_Islam" title="Jesus in Islam">Jesus</a> does not include their names, numbers, or any detailed accounts of their lives. Muslim <a href="/wiki/Exegesis" title="Exegesis">exegesis</a>, however, more or less agrees with the <a href="/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament">New Testament</a> list and says that the disciples included <a href="/wiki/Peter_in_Islam" title="Peter in Islam">Peter</a>, <a href="/wiki/Philip_the_Apostle" title="Philip the Apostle">Philip</a>, <a href="/wiki/Thomas_the_Apostle" title="Thomas the Apostle">Thomas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bartholomew_the_Apostle" title="Bartholomew the Apostle">Bartholomew</a>, <a href="/wiki/Matthew_the_Apostle" title="Matthew the Apostle">Matthew</a>, <a href="/wiki/Andrew_the_Apostle" title="Andrew the Apostle">Andrew</a>, James, <a href="/wiki/Jude_the_Apostle" title="Jude the Apostle">Jude</a>, <a href="/wiki/John_the_Apostle" title="John the Apostle">John</a> and <a href="/wiki/Simon_the_Zealot" title="Simon the Zealot">Simon the Zealot</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENoegelWheeler200386_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENoegelWheeler200386-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFStarkie1965" class="citation book cs1"><a href="/wiki/Walter_Starkie" title="Walter Starkie">Starkie, Walter</a> (1965). <i>The Road to Santiago. The Pilgrims of St. James</i>. Univ. of California Press. <a href="/wiki/OCLC_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="OCLC (identifier)">OCLC</a>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.worldcat.org/oclc/477436336">477436336</a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=The+Road+to+Santiago.+The+Pilgrims+of+St.+James&amp;rft.pub=Univ.+of+California+Press&amp;rft.date=1965&amp;rft_id=info%3Aoclcnum%2F477436336&amp;rft.aulast=Starkie&amp;rft.aufirst=Walter&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJames+the+Great" class="Z3988"></span></li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="External_links">External links</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=James_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: External links">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1134653256">.mw-parser-output .side-box{margin:4px 0;box-sizing:border-box;border:1px solid #aaa;font-size:88%;line-height:1.25em;background-color:#f9f9f9;display:flow-root}.mw-parser-output .side-box-abovebelow,.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{padding:0.25em 0.9em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-image{padding:2px 0 2px 0.9em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-imageright{padding:2px 0.9em 2px 0;text-align:center}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .side-box-flex{display:flex;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{flex:1}}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .side-box{width:238px}.mw-parser-output .side-box-right{clear:right;float:right;margin-left:1em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-left{margin-right:1em}}</style><div class="side-box side-box-right plainlinks sistersitebox"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1126788409">.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul{line-height:inherit;list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0}.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol li,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul li{margin-bottom:0}</style>
<div class="side-box-flex">
<div class="side-box-image"><span class="noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/4a/Commons-logo.svg/30px-Commons-logo.svg.png" decoding="async" width="30" height="40" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/4a/Commons-logo.svg/45px-Commons-logo.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/4a/Commons-logo.svg/59px-Commons-logo.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="1376" /></span></span></div>

</div>
